G. Compliance and governance gate
The DPDP Rules, 2025 were notified with a phased commencement schedule. Multi-city
operations should already use clear processing notices, appropriate security safeguards,
role-scoped access and auditable grievance handling for addresses, schedules, pet
records and provider documents.
The Code on Social Security recognises gig and platform workers, and the Labour Ministry’s
June 2026 aggregator direction states that covered aggregators must electronically share
required worker details through the prescribed framework. Obtain a formal classification
analysis and implement applicable worker-registration/reporting controls before wider
scale.
Consumer flows must provide transparent terms, fair cancellation and an effective
grievance mechanism. The CCPA’s dark-pattern guidance specifically identifies difficult or
hidden subscription cancellation as a subscription trap, and it issued a 2025 self-audit
advisory to e-commerce platforms.
6. Final Phase 10 recommendation
Build:
A city-by-city replication engine that uses mandatory launch gates, small service
clusters, accountable city managers, versioned local pricing, society-led demand,
independent city economics and centrally governed safety, payments and data.
Do
• Prove City 1 before expansion.
• Launch one or two clusters per city.
• Reserve provider and backup capacity.
• Track active supply—not applications.
• Measure GMV, platform revenue and contribution separately.
• Give every public city one accountable owner.

### Page 297

• Keep payments, safety, data and incident rules central.
• Use local society and partner relationships.
• Expand City 3 only after City 2 passes its gate.
• Close weak clusters instead of protecting vanity city counts.
Do not
• Launch five cities from 100 bookings.
• Call a waitlist page an active city.
• advertise city-wide availability from one neighbourhood.
• use one national price for every cluster.
• guarantee one permanent sitter.
• hide city losses inside network totals.
• weaken verification to recruit faster.
• issue franchise rights before the playbook is proven.
• let local teams manage customer money outside the central system.
• count all complaints and safety events under one ambiguous incident rate.

Decision before Phase 11

Final Phase 10 decision: GO—with all five proposed rules approved and the
strengthened Phase 11 gates above.
Phase 11: Mobile App and Advanced Product Phase
Customer App, Sitter App, Tracking, Automation and Product-Scale Architecture
Executive decision
Approve Phase 11, but split it into three controlled product waves:
Wave 1 — Sitter execution app
↓
Wave 2 — Customer mobile app
↓
Wave 3 — Advanced matching, loyalty, health records and automation
The central principle is correct:
Do not build mobile apps to look like a mature startup. Build them when native
capabilities solve measurable operational and retention problems.
The strongest Phase 11 outcome is not “two apps published.” It is:
• Sitter execution becomes more reliable
• Customers receive better visibility and control
• Operations require fewer manual interventions
• Repeated bookings and subscriptions become easier
• Location, payment and personal data remain controlled
• Every advanced feature improves contribution or service quality
1. What changes in Phase 11
Until Phase 10, the operating stack consists mainly of:

### Page 299

• Website or PWA
• Admin dashboard
• Sitter web dashboard
• WhatsApp and SMS communication
• Manual scheduling and escalation
• Semi-automated assignment
• Browser-based subscriptions and payments
This can work during initial validation because human intervention helps discover the
correct operating process.
However, at multi-city scale, several problems usually emerge:
• Sitters miss browser notifications
• Location tracking stops when the browser closes
• Media uploads fail on unstable networks
• Service reports are delayed
• Admins manually chase check-ins
• Customers repeatedly open WhatsApp for updates
• Subscription management is fragmented
• Booking data and real-world execution drift apart
• City teams develop informal workarounds
Phase 11 should turn the proven operating process into a dependable native product
system.

Native-app problem test

• Background or session-based location
• Reliable camera and media capture

4. Why the sitter app should come first
Your recommendation is correct.
Sitter performance affects every transaction
A customer app can improve booking convenience. A sitter app can improve:
• Arrival reliability
• Service evidence
• Location tracking
• Safety escalation
• Report submission
• Backup handling
• Customer communication
• Admin visibility
• Payout accuracy
If sitter execution fails, a polished customer interface only makes the failure more visible.
Native capabilities provide greater immediate value to sitters
A sitter app can provide:
• One-tap accept or reject
• Today’s schedule
• Navigation
• Start-service check-in
• Background route tracking during an active walk

### Page 303

• Photo/video capture
• Offline report drafts
• Incident button
• Customer handover confirmation
• Earnings and payout status
• Leave and availability management
These workflows are more difficult to implement reliably in a browser.
5. Important GPS-tracking rule
Do not track sitters continuously
The correct location model is:
Sitter accepts booking
↓
Sitter travels toward service area
↓
Sitter explicitly starts service
↓
Tracking session begins
↓
Walk or visit is completed
↓
Tracking session stops
↓
Route/report saved
Avoid:
Sitter installs app
↓
Platform tracks location all day
Recommended location states

### Page 304

LOCATION_OFF
PRE_SERVICE_OPTIONAL
ACTIVE_SERVICE_TRACKING
PAUSED
SERVICE_COMPLETED
LOCATION_UPLOAD_PENDING
Permission design
Request location only when the sitter activates the relevant feature.
Apple requires location use to be directly relevant to the app’s function and requires notice
and consent before location data is collected or used. Apple also advises developers to
use background updates only when real-time background information is genuinely needed.
On Android, background location requires a separate permission model; on Android 11 and
later, users generally enable “Allow all the time” from system settings. Google Play permits
background location only when it is important to the app’s core functionality and requires a
declaration, prominent disclosure, privacy policy and review evidence.
Best implementation
Use background location only for an active, user-started walk session.
This produces a defensible core use case:
Tracking the pet’s service route while the sitter performs the booked walk, including when
the screen is locked.
Do not use location for:
• Advertising
• General sitter monitoring
• Attendance outside booked services
• Unrelated analytics
• Persistent employee surveillance
Battery and accuracy
Background location can materially affect battery life, and Android may throttle
background updates. Use:
• Moderate update intervals
• Distance-based updates

### Page 305

• Automatic timeouts
• Immediate stop after service completion
• Upload batching
• Location-quality indicators
• Offline storage with later synchronisation
Android’s official guidance recommends removing updates when no longer needed and
setting reasonable timeouts to prevent continued battery consumption.

7. Offline-first sitter architecture
The sitter app must remain usable when mobile connectivity is weak.
Features that should work offline
• View cached booking details
• Start and stop an assigned service
• Capture location points locally
• Take photographs
• Draft report
• Open safety instructions
• Trigger emergency call actions
• Record incident evidence
Synchronisation states
LOCAL_ONLY
SYNC_PENDING
SYNCING
SYNCED
SYNC_FAILED
CONFLICT_REVIEW
Important controls
• Encrypt local sensitive data
• Store only currently relevant bookings
• Delete cached access details after the service retention window
• Retry uploads automatically
• Show whether the report was actually submitted
• Prevent duplicate completion events
• Reconcile GPS timestamps on the server

### Page 309

• Never mark the booking completed solely because the device displays success

9. Push-notification architecture
Push notifications should improve timeliness, but must not become the system of record.
Recommended events
Customer
• Booking confirmed
• Sitter assigned
• Sitter en route
• Walk started
• Service completed
• Report available
• Schedule changed
• Payment failed
• Renewal upcoming
• Credit expiring
• Safety/support update
Sitter
• New booking
• Acceptance deadline
• Upcoming booking
• Travel reminder
• Check-in overdue
• Report pending
• Backup request
• Payout processed
• Document expiring
• Support message
Firebase Cloud Messaging provides cross-platform message delivery for Android, iOS, web
and Flutter clients. Apple uses APNs for remote notification delivery.

### Page 312

Notifications still require user permission. Android 13 and later require the
POST_NOTIFICATIONS runtime permission for most notifications, while iOS requires
notification authorisation before alerts, sounds or badges are shown.
Notification design rules
• Ask permission after explaining the value
• Let users control marketing separately
• Do not include sensitive home or pet-health details in lock-screen text
• Deep-link to the relevant booking
• Store every important event in the app inbox
• Use WhatsApp/SMS or support calls for critical operational fallback
• Never assume push delivery alone proves the user was informed
10. AI-assisted sitter matching
Correct use of AI
AI should recommend and rank candidates.
It should not autonomously assign every pet without operational constraints.
Booking request
↓
Hard eligibility filters
↓
Capacity and geography filters
↓
Risk and skill filters
↓
AI/rules-based ranking
↓
Recommended sitters
↓
Automatic or human confirmation based on risk

### Page 313

Hard filters before AI scoring
A sitter must first satisfy:
• Active verification
• Correct service permission
• Correct city/zone
• Available time
• Travel limit
• Pet-size capability
• Behaviour-risk eligibility
• Society access
• Capacity
• No quality suspension
AI must never rank an ineligible sitter above an eligible one.
Suggested ranking factors
Factor Example weight
Pet compatibility 20
Previous relationship 20
Reliability 15
Distance/travel time 15
Availability fit 10
Customer preference 10
Rating and report quality 5
Backup availability 5
High-risk cases requiring human approval
• Aggressive pet history
• Senior/medical care
• Medication

### Page 314

• First boarding
• Previous incident
• Multiple large pets
• Special-access home
• Emergency booking
• Very young animals
Explainability
The operations team should see reasons such as:
Recommended because Riya has completed seven services with Bruno, has 98%
punctuality, is 1.2 km away and has matching morning availability.
Avoid an unexplained “AI score: 92.”
11. Loyalty system
Do not begin with complex points and tiers.
Start with behaviour-linked benefits
Reward:
• Completed repeat bookings
• Subscription renewal
• Referrals after completed service
• On-time customer handover
• Honest reviews
• Society programme participation
Early loyalty model
Care Credits
- Earn after completed eligible service
- Apply to future service
- Non-transferable
- No cash value
- Expiry clearly disclosed

### Page 315

- Refund and reversal rules visible
Avoid
• Points for app opens
• Gamifying emergency or medical care
• Unlimited liability
• Confusing conversion rates
• Hidden expiry
• Rewards before service completion
• Referral abuse
12. Wallet versus service credits
Use the word credits rather than “wallet” in the initial release.
Recommended design:
• Non-transferable
• No cash withdrawal
• No peer-to-peer transfer
• Used only for eligible platform bookings
• Issued for subscription entitlement, refund, promotion or service recovery
• Separate balances by credit type
• Full transaction history
RBI distinguishes closed-system instruments used only to buy the issuer’s own goods or
services from broader prepaid payment instruments. Because your marketplace may
facilitate third-party service providers, a reloadable customer-funded balance can create
additional regulatory questions. Obtain payments counsel before creating cash-like stored
value, transferability or third-party merchant acceptance.
Recommended Phase 11 approach
Launch:
• Subscription service credits
• Promotional credits
• Refund credits with customer choice where lawful

### Page 316

• Loyalty credits
Defer:
• User-funded cash wallet
• Peer transfer
• Cash-out
• Cross-merchant usage
• Stored balances unrelated to an active service obligation
13. Pet health records
The app may become the owner-controlled record layer for:
• Vaccinations
• Allergies
• Medication instructions
• Veterinary consultation documents
• Lab reports
• Medical conditions
• Surgery history
• Follow-up reminders
Access design
Role Access
Pet parent Full record
Assigned sitter Only care information necessary for current service
Vet partner Records explicitly shared for the consultation
Support No routine clinical access
Society/RWA No access
Marketing No access
Admin Restricted and audited
Safety rules

### Page 317

• AI does not provide final diagnosis
• Support does not interpret medical reports
• Sitters see only operationally necessary instructions
• Every document share is logged
• Owners can revoke access
• Records have retention and deletion policies
• Urgent symptoms route to a vet, not an automated diagnosis
Location history, home schedules, provider records and pet-health documents require
clear notices, access controls, security safeguards and grievance/deletion handling under
the Indian data-protection framework. The DPDP Rules, 2025 were notified with phased
commencement.
14. Account and privacy controls
Both apps should include:
• Download personal data
• Update profile information
• Communication preferences
• Location-permission status
• Active devices
• Logout from other devices
• Delete account
• Request data correction
• Request support
• Privacy policy
• Retention explanation
Apple requires apps that support account creation to let users initiate account deletion
inside the app. Google Play requires a readily discoverable deletion option inside the app
and a corresponding external web deletion path.
The deletion system must distinguish:
• Deleting the app

### Page 318

• Deactivating the account
• Cancelling a subscription
• Deleting the account and eligible associated data
These are different actions.
15. Do not build a basic wrapper
The proposed “fast app build in 2–3 months” is acceptable only for:
• Internal testing
• Sitter beta
• Limited native proof of concept
It is not a strong public app strategy if the result is just the website inside a mobile shell.
Apple’s App Review Guidelines state that apps should provide features, content and UI
beyond a repackaged website.
A public mobile app should add real native value
At minimum:
• Native push
• Offline workflows
• Native camera/media capture
• Secure device sessions
• Native location during active services
• Deep linking
• Biometric unlock
• Smooth repeated booking
• Background upload/retry
• App-specific accessibility and navigation
16. Recommended technology strategy
One backend, multiple clients
Core API platform

### Page 319

├── Admin web application
├── Customer web/PWA
├── Customer mobile app
└── Sitter mobile app
Do not create separate business logic in every client.
Keep server-side:
• Pricing
• Eligibility
• Matching rules
• Capacity
• Booking status transitions
• Credit ledger
• Payment verification
• Refund rules
• Incident state
• Access control
• Subscription lifecycle
The apps should not be trusted to decide:
• Whether a payment succeeded
• Whether a sitter is eligible
• How many credits remain
• Whether a booking is completed
• Whether a refund is owed
• Whether an incident can be closed
17. Cross-platform versus native
Cross-platform is appropriate when
• The team is strong in TypeScript/Dart

### Page 320

• Most features share common UI and logic
• Faster simultaneous iOS/Android delivery matters
• Native bridges for GPS, camera, push and background jobs are tested carefully
Fully native may be justified when
• Background location is highly complex
• Device-level performance is critical
• The team already has strong iOS and Android specialists
• Platform-specific workflows differ significantly
• Reliability outweighs initial development speed
Practical recommendation
Use a well-supported cross-platform framework for both apps, but treat these as native-
critical modules:
• Location
• Background service/session
• Push
• Camera and uploads
• Secure storage
• Biometrics
• Offline database
• Deep links
Do not choose the framework solely from developer familiarity. Run a technical proof of
concept for:
1. Screen locked during a 45-minute walk
2. Location retained with expected battery use
3. Network lost and restored
4. Photo/report upload retry
5. Push received across device states
6. App killed and reopened
7. Permission denied and later enabled

### Page 329

Final Phase 11 recommendation
Build:
A sitter-first mobile operating system that captures reliable check-ins, active-service
location, offline reports, incidents and payouts—followed by a customer app focused
on booking, live visibility, subscriptions, credits and pet-care records.
Do not:
• Build two giant apps simultaneously
• Track sitters continuously
• Request every permission during onboarding
• Put business rules only in the apps
• Let AI override eligibility and safety controls
• Build a cash-like wallet without regulatory analysis
• Hide account deletion
• abandon the website/PWA
• launch loyalty before core fulfilment works
• treat app downloads as the primary success metric
Phase 11 win condition
Mobile products reduce operational effort and service failures while increasing
customer retention, without weakening privacy, battery performance, payment
accuracy or sitter adoption.
Phase 11 status: GO—start with the sitter app, validate it operationally, then launch
the customer app to repeat customers and subscribers.
Phase 11 Product Priority and Mobile Architecture
Executive decision
Approve the sitter-first strategy and React Native with Expo recommendation.
However, the proposed priority list needs two changes:
1. Advanced GPS is not a separate fourth-stage product. Session-based tracking is
part of the sitter-app foundation.
2. Credits, loyalty and wallet must be separated. Service credits can launch early;
loyalty comes later; a cash-like wallet should be deferred.
Corrected build order

4. Advanced GPS belongs inside the sitter-app foundation
Correct tracking model
Track only during a user-initiated service session:
Sitter taps “Start Walk”
↓
Tracking begins
↓
App enters background if necessary
↓
Location points are stored locally
↓
Points sync to the server
↓
Sitter taps “Complete”
↓
Tracking stops
Expo supports background tasks and background-location updates through its
TaskManager and Location APIs, but operating-system constraints still apply. Expo’s
documentation notes, for example, that background location stops if the user terminates
the app and resumes only after reopening it.
Never use continuous workforce surveillance
Do not track:
• Outside an accepted booking
• Throughout the sitter’s day
• During leave
• After service completion
• For advertising

### Page 339

• Without clear disclosure
GPS session schema
location_sessions
- id
- booking_id
- sitter_id
- device_id
- started_at
- stopped_at
- status
- tracking_reason
- consent_version
- point_count
- distance_meters
- quality_score
location_points
- id
- session_id
- recorded_at
- latitude
- longitude
- accuracy_meters
- speed
- heading
- source
- sync_status
GPS quality indicators
The customer should not see an apparently precise route when the location signal is poor.
Possible statuses:

### Page 340

• Accurate
• Approximate
• Temporarily unavailable
• Device offline
• Upload pending
• Route incomplete
GPS acceptance criteria
• Tracking starts only after explicit sitter action
• Tracking stops automatically after completion
• Offline points sync later
• Duplicate points are handled
• Poor-accuracy points are filtered or marked
• Battery impact is measured on real devices
• No location remains active after the booking
• Customer sees only the relevant booking route

7. Priority 4: Matching engine before “AI matching”
Do not begin by training a complex model.
Build matching in three stages.
Stage 1: Deterministic eligibility
Remove every sitter who fails a hard requirement:
• Wrong city or zone

### Page 345

• Outside travel limit
• Unavailable
• No required service permission
• Incompatible pet-risk level
• Suspended
• No society access
• Capacity exhausted
• Required documents expired
Stage 2: Rules-based ranking
Rank remaining sitters using explainable factors:
Factor Suggested weight
Previous relationship with pet 20
Pet/skill compatibility 20
Travel time 15
Reliability 15
Schedule fit 10
Customer preference 10
Rating/report quality 5
Backup availability 5
Stage 3: AI-assisted ranking
Once sufficient historical data exists, a model can estimate:
• Acceptance probability
• On-time probability
• Completion probability
• Customer satisfaction
• Repeat-sitter likelihood
• Cancellation risk

### Page 346

The model should generate recommendations—not bypass safety filters.
Explainable output
Operations should see:
Recommended because Riya has completed eight services with Bruno, has 98%
punctuality, is 1.4 km away and has matching weekday availability.
Not:
AI score: 94.
Human approval required
• Behaviourally high-risk pets
• Medication
• Senior or medical care
• First boarding
• Previous incident
• Emergency request
• Multiple large pets
• Unusual property access
8. Priority 5: Credits, loyalty and wallet
These are three different products.
A. Service credits — early
Used for:
• Walking-plan entitlements
• Starter packs
• Service recovery
• Eligible refunds
• Promotions
Required ledger:
GRANT
RESERVE

### Page 347

CONSUME
RELEASE
RESTORE
EXPIRE
ROLLOVER
Service credits should already exist before the customer app because subscription
fulfilment depends on them.
B. Loyalty — later
Reward meaningful behaviour:
• Completed repeat service
• Subscription renewal
• Successful referral
• Society participation
• Long-term retention
Do not reward app opens or encourage unnecessary pet services.
C. Cash-like wallet — defer
Do not initially build a customer-funded, transferable stored-value wallet.
Use:
• Non-transferable service credits
• No cash withdrawal
• No peer-to-peer transfer
• Clear expiry
• Full transaction history
• Usage only for eligible platform services
This reduces product complexity and financial-regulatory risk.

Do not expose individual employee or resident care information to organisations.

Product Platform Status

12. React Native with Expo vs Flutter
Option A: React Native with Expo
Advantages for your context
• TypeScript and React knowledge transfers directly
• Easier alignment with a Next.js team
• Shared domain types and API clients
• Faster hiring from JavaScript/React talent pools
• Expo provides an integrated framework and native-device APIs
• Native modules can still be added where required
React Native’s own setup documentation recommends using a framework for new
applications, and Expo provides a React Native framework with routing, build tooling and
native-device modules.
Risks

### Page 351

• Background-location behaviour still varies by operating system
• Some native libraries require development builds or custom configuration
• Poorly selected third-party packages can cause upgrade friction
• JavaScript knowledge does not remove the need to understand iOS and Android
lifecycle behaviour
Expo’s current tooling supports React Native’s newer architecture, but third-party-library
compatibility should still be checked before adoption.
Option B: Flutter
Advantages
• Strong UI consistency
• Highly controlled rendering
• Single Dart codebase
• Mature architecture guidance
• Excellent for custom interfaces and motion-heavy applications
Flutter officially positions itself as a framework for natively compiled multiplatform
applications from one codebase and publishes detailed scalable-app architecture
guidance.
Costs for your context
• Requires Dart expertise
• Less direct code and knowledge reuse from Next.js/React
• Separate frontend hiring profile
• Shared TypeScript domain packages cannot be consumed directly
• Web and mobile teams may diverge more quickly
Final framework decision
Use React Native with Expo when:
• Your existing stack is React/Next.js
• Your team is strongest in TypeScript
• Speed and shared engineering practices matter

### Page 352

• You are prepared to test native-critical modules thoroughly
Use Flutter when:
• You have a dedicated Flutter team
• Visual consistency is the dominant requirement
• You accept a separate language and mobile architecture
• You are not optimising for React/TypeScript reuse
For your current full-stack profile, React Native with Expo is the stronger default.
13. Corrected React Native stack
Mobile framework
• React Native
• Expo with development builds
• Expo Router
• TypeScript
Expo Router provides file-based routing and native, deep-linkable navigation across
Android, iOS and web.
Do not rely only on Expo Go for production development. Features such as production
push notifications require development builds, and native/background behaviour must be
tested in real app binaries.
Backend
• Existing Node.js/Next.js API layer
• PostgreSQL
• Existing payment and subscription services
• Background workers/queues
• Object storage
• WebSocket or event-stream layer where justified
Authentication
Use the same identity system, but model mobile sessions separately:
user
device

### Page 353

mobile_session
push_token
refresh_token
last_seen_at
app_version
Store small secrets or tokens in secure device storage. Expo SecureStore provides
encrypted local key-value storage; it should not be used as a general database for large
payloads.
Navigation
• Expo Router
• Role-separated route groups
• Deep links from notifications
• Auth-protected layouts
Offline database
• Expo SQLite
• Local outbox queue
• Conflict-resolution layer
Expo SQLite persists its database across app restarts and is suitable as the local
persistence layer for offline-first workflows.
Use it for:
• Assigned booking cache
• Pending service events
• Location points
• Unsynced reports
• Media-upload queue
Do not store highly sensitive records indefinitely on the device.
Push notifications
Recommended starting choice:
• expo-notifications
• Expo Push Service initially

• Direct FCM/APNs later if more control is required
Expo’s notifications API is push-service agnostic, so the client can later use native device
tokens for direct FCM/APNs delivery.
Location and background tasks
• expo-location
• expo-task-manager
• Development-build testing
• Active-service sessions only
Maps
Evaluate:
• Google Maps
• Mappls
• Possibly separate maps for display and address intelligence
Mappls currently offers React Native mapping and live-location SDK documentation,
making it a credible India-focused candidate. Selection should be based on address
accuracy, route quality, geocoding, pricing, support and compatibility with your chosen
Expo development-build workflow.
Run a proof of concept using real launch areas:
• Whitefield
• Baner
• Powai
• Gurugram sectors
• Bopal
• Vesu
Do not select a map provider from screenshots alone.
Media
Use:
• Native camera/image picker
• Local compression
• Temporary file queue

### Page 355

• Signed uploads
• Object storage such as S3-compatible storage or Cloudinary
Expo provides camera, image-picker and filesystem modules suitable for capturing and
temporarily storing media before upload.
Analytics
Recommended:
• PostHog for product analytics, funnels and feature flags
• Firebase Analytics as an alternative or supplementary acquisition layer
• Strict event redaction
PostHog’s React Native SDK supports Expo projects, custom events and feature flags.
Do not send:
• Customer addresses
• Access codes

• Incident narratives
to general analytics tools.
Error and performance monitoring
• Sentry
• Release tracking
• Source maps
• Navigation performance

Sentry provides dedicated React Native and Expo integrations, including Expo Router
instrumentation.

Final Phase 11 product doctrine
First digitise the work that creates trust. Then digitise the convenience that retains
customers. Only after both work should the platform add intelligence, loyalty and
deeper records.
Official Phase 11 focus

after the operational core is stable.
Phase 11 Products 1–2: Mobile Apps and Advanced GPS System
Executive decision
Both product definitions are strong, with four important corrections:
1. GPS tracking belongs inside the sitter-app P0, not as a later standalone feature.
2. The sitter must not see more customer data than the current booking requires.
3. The emergency button must use multiple escalation channels—not push or
WhatsApp alone.
4. Route and timing alerts should open operational reviews, not automatically
accuse a sitter of misconduct.
The complete system should operate as:
Sitter app
↓ emits verified service events
Operations command centre
↓ monitors exceptions
Customer app
↓ displays trusted service state
Central backend
↓ controls bookings, payments, credits and incidents
Product 1: Sitter Mobile App
1. Product goal
The sitter app should make field delivery:
• More punctual
• More documentable

### Page 365

• Easier to monitor
• Safer during incidents
• Less dependent on WhatsApp
• More transparent for sitters
• Easier to reconcile financially
The sitter application is not merely a provider profile or job board.
It is the field-operations application through which a scheduled digital booking
becomes a verified physical service.

• Payout mismatch
• Personal emergency
• Temporarily unavailable
• Other

10. Arrival and start-service flow
Recommended start flow
Sitter approaches location
↓
App checks permission and GPS quality
↓
Sitter taps “I’ve Arrived”
↓
System compares position with service geofence
↓
Handover confirmed
↓
Sitter taps “Start Service”
↓
Active tracking and timer begin
Start-service preconditions
• Booking is assigned to this sitter
• Current time is within allowed range
• Required location permission exists
• Device has acceptable location signal

### Page 375

• Handover is confirmed
• No conflicting active service exists
• Safety instructions acknowledged
Starting outside the geofence
Do not automatically block every legitimate case.
Allow an exception with a reason:
• Customer requested another meeting point
• Society entrance differs from residence
• GPS is inaccurate
• Admin authorised start
• Emergency relocation
The exception should be logged and surfaced for review.
Android geofencing uses a latitude, longitude and radius to identify entry or exit from an
area, but geofence events and background location are subject to platform timing and
accuracy constraints.
11. GPS session architecture
Session-based tracking only
Booking assigned
↓
No continuous location collection
↓
Sitter starts eligible service
↓
Tracking session activated
↓
Location points collected during service
↓
Service ends

### Page 376

↓
Tracking stopped automatically
Do not track sitters continuously throughout the working day.
Apple permits background services only for their intended purposes and requires location
use to be directly relevant to app functionality. Android requires appropriate location
permissions, and apps targeting Android 14 or later must declare the correct foreground-
service type for a location foreground service.
Important Expo limitation
Expo supports background location through its Location and TaskManager APIs, but
background updates remain subject to platform constraints. Expo states that background
location stops if the user terminates the application and resumes after the app is restarted.
Therefore, your product must detect and report:
• App terminated
• Background permission removed
• Location disabled
• Tracking task stopped
• Network unavailable
• Points pending upload
Do not falsely label these situations as a completed, continuously tracked route.
12. Adaptive tracking strategy
Do not hard-code one aggressive interval for every device and service.
Use adaptive profiles:
Walking profile
• Higher frequency while moving
• Lower frequency while stationary
• Distance-based updates
• Batch upload during weak connectivity
• Immediate upload for safety events
Sitting profile

### Page 377

Continuous route tracking is usually unnecessary.
Use:
• Arrival verification
• Periodic check-ins
• Service start/end
• Media and report evidence
Boarding profile
Do not continuously track the host.
Use:
• Check-in/check-out
• Scheduled care updates
• Media
• Incident reporting
Grooming profile
Use:
• Arrival
• Start
• Before media
• End
• After media
• Report
Android recommends limiting location work to what the feature needs because
background location affects battery usage; Google’s location stack includes battery-
optimised fused-location and geofencing capabilities.

14. Offline tracking and report sync
Local outbox
The sitter app should locally queue:
• Start-service event

• Media files
• Observations

Server rules
• Events require unique idempotency keys

### Page 380

• Duplicate completion must be ignored
• Device timestamps must be preserved
• Server-received timestamps must also be stored
• Conflicts should not silently overwrite records
• Media upload failure must not erase the written report
• Earnings should remain pending until completion is validated
15. Photo and video upload
Capture rules
• Compress media before upload
• Strip unnecessary metadata where appropriate
• Use signed upload URLs
• Store upload progress
• Retry failed uploads
• Show pending versus uploaded status
• Obtain customer consent for routine media
• Avoid showing unrelated people or private home areas
Suggested limits
• A small number of meaningful photographs
• Short video clips only when useful
• No forced high-resolution upload
• Media retained according to a documented policy
Location routes, customer homes and pet-related health or behaviour notes should not be
sent to general-purpose analytics or error logs.
16. End-service validation
When the sitter taps End Service, evaluate:
• Minimum expected duration
• GPS/session health

### Page 381

• Distance where relevant
• Required observations
• Required media
• Open incident state
• Customer handover confirmation
Early-end flow
Early end detected
↓
Sitter selects reason
↓
Operations notified
↓
Service remains “Pending Review”
↓
Customer receives neutral update
↓
Admin validates completion and credit/payout
Do not immediately accuse the sitter or automatically release full payout.
Valid reasons may include:
• Pet became unwell
• Weather emergency
• Customer requested early return
• Unsafe animal encountered
• Service changed by operations
• GPS malfunction
• Incident opened

A sitter-facing reliability score can help performance, but it must be transparent and
contestable.
Recommended factors
• On-time arrival
• Accepted-booking completion
• Sitter-initiated cancellation
• No-shows
• Report completion
• Customer rating
• Incident outcomes
• Training/document status
Do not penalise
• Customer cancellation
• Admin reassignment
• App outage
• Verified GPS failure
• Medical emergency
• Prompt, valid rejection before acceptance

• Improvement actions
• Dispute route
• Review period

Advanced GPS Tracking
31. Correct GPS architecture
Sitter initiates service
↓
Permission and device-state check
↓
Server creates location session
↓
Foreground/background location begins
↓
Points collected adaptively
↓
Points stored locally
↓
Near-real-time batches sent to backend
↓
Customer and admin receive session state
↓
Alerts evaluate session events

### Page 397

↓
Service completion requested
↓
Backend validates and closes session
↓
Route summary generated
On Android, active background location normally requires the correct location foreground-
service configuration and visible foreground-service behaviour; Android 13 and later also
introduced runtime notification permission.
On iOS, the app must request appropriate location authorisation and configure background
location for the actual use case.
32. Permission UX
Do not request background location immediately after login.
Recommended sequence
Sitter accepts first tracked walk
↓
App explains why location is needed
↓
Foreground permission requested
↓
Background-service explanation shown
↓
Background access requested/configured
↓
Test confirms tracking readiness
For notifications:
User sees value explanation
↓
System permission requested

### Page 398

Android 13+ uses the POST_NOTIFICATIONS runtime permission, while Apple requires
explicit notification authorisation through UNUserNotificationCenter.
33. GPS alert engine
Alerts should use:
• Static rules
• Contextual thresholds
• Historical baselines
• GPS quality
• Service type
• Pet requirements
• Human review
Do not trigger based on a single noisy GPS point.
Alert state model
DETECTED
OBSERVING
CONFIRMED
NOTIFIED
ACKNOWLEDGED
RESOLVED
FALSE_POSITIVE
ESCALATED
34. Late-start alert
Trigger inputs
• Scheduled start
• Allowed grace period
• Sitter arrival status
• Current travel estimate

### Page 399

• Customer reschedule
• Society gate delay
• Weather/traffic disruption
Escalation
Approaching start and sitter not en route
↓
Sitter reminder
↓
Grace period exceeded
↓
Operations alert
↓
Backup assessment
↓
Customer update
Do not wait until the appointment is already severely late before evaluating backup
capacity.
35. Wrong-location alert
Recommended logic
Open a review when:
• Start occurs outside the configured radius
• GPS accuracy is acceptable
• No authorised meeting-point exception exists
• Several points confirm the location
Avoid false positives from
• Apartment entrance versus tower
• Indoor GPS drift
• Dense urban buildings

### Page 400

• Customer-requested park meetup
• Inaccurate device location
The app should first ask the sitter to confirm:
You appear to be outside the expected start area. Are you meeting the customer at another
location?
36. Route-anomaly alert
“Unusual route” requires careful definition.
Possible signals
• Route goes far outside expected service zone
• Sudden impossible movement
• Repeated circular GPS noise
• Service leaves approved boundaries
• Route deviates materially from historical pattern
• Pet-risk rule prohibits certain area
Response
A route anomaly should:
• Be labelled as a review signal
• Prompt sitter confirmation
• Show GPS-quality context
• Notify operations only after persistence
• Avoid alarming the customer prematurely
Never send:
Your sitter is taking your dog somewhere unsafe.
based only on an automated route deviation.
37. Long-pause alert
Context matters
No movement may be acceptable when:

### Page 401

• Pet rests
• Sitter gives water
• Traffic crossing is delayed
• Customer requested a stationary outdoor break
• GPS signal is weak
• Service is pet sitting rather than walking
Recommended rule
Use:
• No significant movement
• For a sustained period
• During a walking service
• With acceptable GPS accuracy
• Without a pause reason
First prompt the sitter:
You have been stationary for several minutes. Is everything okay?
Escalate only when the sitter fails to respond or another risk signal exists.
38. Early-end alert
Trigger
• Completed before minimum acceptable duration
• No approved exception
• GPS/session is valid
• No incident explains the end
Workflow
Early completion requested
↓
Reason required
↓
Booking marked Pending Review

### Page 402

↓
Operations evaluates
↓
Credit and payout resolved
Do not automatically deduct the sitter’s payout or customer credit.
39. Tracking-lost alert
Possible causes
• Permission revoked
• GPS disabled
• App force-closed
• Device battery exhausted
• Operating-system restriction
• Network unavailable
• Expo background task stopped
• App crash
Required response
1. Attempt local tracking continuity.
2. Notify sitter in-app if possible.
3. Record session degradation.
4. Alert admin after threshold.
5. Inform customer neutrally if live tracking is materially interrupted.
6. Preserve service evidence from timer, media and reports.
Customer copy:
Live location is temporarily unavailable. The service remains in progress, and operations
has been notified.
Not:
Sitter stopped tracking.

### Page 403

40. Emergency alert
Emergency Help should override ordinary alert handling.
Immediate actions
• Create critical incident
• Attach last known location
• Lock the booking from normal completion
• Notify city operations
• Notify central incident team
• Offer direct call
• Display safety instructions
• Start acknowledgement timer
• Escalate to backup contacts
If the device is offline, the app should:
• Store the incident locally
• Present direct call options
• Attempt SMS/phone fallback
• Sync evidence when connectivity returns

Product doctrine

### Page 410

The sitter app should prove what happened, support the person delivering the service
and surface exceptions early. The customer app should translate that operational
evidence into confidence, visibility and convenient repeat care.
Phase 11 Product 1–2 win condition
At least 95% of eligible services are digitally acknowledged, started, monitored and
reported, while customers gain live visibility without creating continuous worker
surveillance, excessive battery use or unsafe dependence on automated alerts.

Product 3: AI-Assisted Sitter Matching
1. Product objective
The matching system should reduce:
• Manual search time
• Poor distance assignments
• Skill mismatches
• Schedule conflicts
• Repeated sitter changes
• Last-minute cancellations
• Subscription fulfilment risk
It should not remove human accountability.

### Page 412

NIST’s AI-risk guidance recommends defining where human oversight is necessary,
documenting system limitations, providing understandable outputs and continuously
monitoring performance and harmful outcomes. That is especially relevant here because
matching affects both pet safety and sitter earning opportunities.
2. Why full auto-assignment is premature
A pet-care assignment contains context that may not be completely represented in
structured data:
• The dog recently became reactive
• The customer distrusts a particular replacement
• A society gate has changed its rules
• A sitter has accepted too many consecutive walks
• A medical or behavioural note is ambiguous
• A location appears close geographically but is difficult to reach
• The sitter is technically available but is the only backup for another subscription
An automated system may produce a mathematically plausible match that is operationally
unsafe.
Recommended responsibility
Actor Responsibility
System Filters ineligible sitters
Ranking engine Orders eligible candidates
Admin Reviews context and confirms
Sitter Accepts the assignment
Customer Confirms where appropriate
Quality team Audits results and failures
3. Three-stage matching architecture
Stage 1: Hard eligibility filters
Before any AI scoring, eliminate sitters who do not satisfy mandatory conditions.

### Page 413

Hard filters
• Sitter account is active
• Required documents are current
• Correct service permission exists
• Correct city and service zone
• Available at requested time
• Travel time is feasible
• Daily capacity is available
• Pet-risk level is permitted
• Sitter has required skills
• Society access is valid
• No overlapping booking
• Sitter is not paused or under investigation
• Required backup coverage remains available
All sitters
↓
Hard safety and operational rules
↓
Eligible candidate pool
The AI should never “outscore” a failed safety rule.
Stage 2: Candidate ranking
Rank only the eligible pool.
Recommended factors
Factor Suggested importance
Previous relationship with pet Very high
Pet-risk and skill compatibility Very high
Availability fit Very high

### Page 414

Factor Suggested importance
Travel time High
Reliability High
Service-specific experience High
Customer preference Medium-high
Society access familiarity Medium-high
Rating and report quality Medium
Price/payout compatibility Medium
Backup implications Medium
Example scoring model
Match score = 20%×pet compatibility
+20%×repeat relationship
+15%×reliability
+15%×travel fit
+10%×availability fit
+10%×customer preference
+5%×service quality
+5%×backup health
Weights should vary by service.
For example:
• Dog walking: distance, punctuality and previous pet relationship matter strongly.
• Boarding: environment, trial history and household compatibility matter more.
• Senior care: relevant skill and medical-instruction reliability dominate.
• Grooming: package skill, pet size and equipment matter more than distance alone.
4. Do not call an arbitrary score a probability
Avoid:
Riya — 92% probability of being the right sitter.
unless your model has been calibrated against real outcomes and 92% has a statistically
validated meaning.

### Page 415

Use:
Riya — Match score 92/100
or:
Excellent operational match
Suggested bands
Score Label
90–100 Excellent match
80–89 Strong match
70–79 Acceptable match
60–69 Manual review required
Below 60 Do not recommend automatically
5. Admin recommendation interface
Recommended output
Recommended sitters
1. Riya — 92/100 · Excellent match
• 1.2 km from the customer
• Previously completed seven walks with Bruno
• Qualified for large-dog handling
• Available at 7:00 AM
• 98% on-time rate
• Approved for this society

### Page 416

• Available at requested time

• 2.4 km away

• Available within a 30-minute flexible window
Admin actions
• Assign
• View complete profile
• Compare candidates
• Request sitter acceptance
• Select another candidate
• Record override reason
• Escalate for specialist review
6. Admin override system
Whenever the admin ignores the top recommendation, request a structured reason:
• Customer requested another sitter
• Existing pet relationship
• Risk information not represented
• Society access issue
• Schedule/travel concern
• Backup-capacity concern
• Sitter workload concern
• Quality concern
• Customer gender preference where legitimately applicable
• Other
This information becomes valuable training data.

### Page 417

Do not automatically treat the administrator’s choice as “correct.” Compare it with the
actual outcome.

Every recommendation should store its algorithm version so historical decisions remain
auditable after scoring logic changes.
9. Matching APIs
POST /v1/admin/matching/requests

### Page 420

GET /v1/admin/matching/requests/{id}/recommendations
POST /v1/admin/matching/requests/{id}/assign
POST /v1/admin/matching/requests/{id}/override
GET /v1/admin/matching/requests/{id}/explanation
POST /v1/admin/matching/requests/{id}/outcome
Example response:
{
"algorithm_version": "matching-v1.3",
"candidates": [
{
"sitter_id": "sitter_riya",
"match_score": 92,
"label": "excellent_match",
"reasons": [
"Completed 7 previous services with this pet",
"1.2 km estimated distance",
"Qualified for large dogs",
"Available at requested time",
"98% on-time rate"
],
"warnings": []
}
]
}
10. Matching fairness and worker controls
Matching can affect how many earning opportunities each sitter receives. Therefore, avoid
creating a system that permanently sends all work to a small number of already successful
providers.

### Page 421

Track:
• Recommendations per sitter
• Assignments per sitter
• Acceptance rate
• Earnings distribution
• New-sitter exposure
• Geographic distribution
• Admin overrides
• False exclusions
• Rating bias
• Customer-preference effects
NIST recommends defining human roles, measuring errors and negative effects, monitoring
performance over time and maintaining processes to correct systems that exceed
acceptable risk levels.
New sitter cold-start policy
A new sitter cannot have:
• Past relationship history
• Many ratings
• Large completed-service volume
Create a controlled trial mechanism:
• Low-risk first assignments
• Supervised or monitored first services
• Explicit “new approved sitter” status
• No automatic penalty for limited history
• Progressive capacity increase
11. Matching KPIs
Metric Purpose
Admin recommendation acceptance Recommendation usefulness

### Page 422

Metric Purpose
Sitter acceptance rate Operational fit
Assignment time Efficiency
On-time performance Service quality
Completion rate Match viability
Repeat-sitter request Relationship quality
Customer rating Customer outcome
Override rate Model/rule limitations
Incident rate Safety
Candidate concentration Fairness and supply health
Matching-related support tickets Operational clarity
Initial targets
• Top-three recommendation contains final selected sitter: 80%+
• Admin accepts top recommendation: 50–70% initially
• Eligible-candidate false exclusions: near zero
• Human review: 100% initially
• High-risk booking auto-assignment: zero
Product 4: Loyalty, Credits and Referral System
12. Separate the five balance types
Do not combine every balance into one number called “wallet.”
Balance Purpose Recommended status
Subscription service credits Included walks/services Launch
Referral credits Earned from eligible referrals Launch
Promotional credits Marketing campaigns Launch carefully

### Page 423

Balance Purpose Recommended status
Launch where
Society benefit credits Resident/company benefits
applicable
Refund/service-recovery Customer-selected or goodwill
Controlled
credits credit
Customer-funded cash wallet Stored monetary value Defer
13. Why the “cash wallet” should be deferred
RBI defines a closed-system instrument as one used to purchase goods or services from
the issuing entity only; it cannot be used to settle third-party services and does not permit
cash withdrawal. A marketplace using independent sitters, groomers or other providers
should not automatically assume that a customer-funded wallet qualifies as an
unregulated closed system.
Therefore, avoid initially building:
• User-reloadable money balances
• Peer-to-peer transfers
• Cash withdrawal
• Transferable balances
• Payments outside your platform
• Cross-merchant acceptance
• Wallet-to-bank settlement
Before introducing those features, obtain specialist Indian payments advice regarding PPI
and marketplace-payment treatment.
14. Recommended early credit system
Use non-transferable ledgered credits.
Credit categories
SUBSCRIPTION
REFERRAL
PROMOTIONAL

### Page 424

SOCIETY_BENEFIT
SERVICE_RECOVERY
REFUND_ELECTED
MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT
Credit states
AVAILABLE
RESERVED
CONSUMED
RELEASED
EXPIRED
REVERSED
FROZEN
Core rule
Never store only:
customer.wallet_balance = 450
Use an immutable ledger and calculate the balance from transactions.
15. Credit ledger
credit_accounts
- id

GRANT

Balance calculation
Available balance = grants+releases−reserves−consumption−reversals−expiry
16. Refund-credit rule
Do not automatically replace every monetary refund with platform credit.
Recommended options:
• Refund to original payment method
• Customer voluntarily selects faster platform credit
• Goodwill/service-recovery credit added separately
• Contractually valid non-cash benefit where clearly disclosed
The customer should see:
• Original amount

### Page 426

• Refund method
• Credit amount
• Expiry
• Eligible services
• Whether the decision can be changed
Indian dark-pattern guidance prohibits deceptive design, including subscription traps and
hidden or unnecessarily complex cancellation practices. The same product principle
should apply to credit expiry and refund choice: terms must be visible before acceptance
and not designed to trap value.
17. Referral programme
Recommended offer
Give ₹150, Get ₹150
But define the ₹150 as an eligible platform credit—not withdrawable cash.
End-to-end flow
Existing customer receives referral code
↓
Customer shares code
↓
Friend signs up through code
↓
Friend completes first eligible paid service
↓
Refund/reversal window passes
↓
Friend discount is finalised
↓
Referrer receives ₹150 referral credit
Do not grant the reward merely because:
• The friend created an account

### Page 427

• The friend entered a code
• A payment attempt occurred
• A booking was cancelled
• A fully refunded service was completed
• The same person created another account
18. Referral eligibility rules
Recommended conditions:
• New paying customer
• First eligible completed service
• Minimum booking value
• No full refund
• Unique verified phone/account
• Referral code applied before checkout
• One referrer per new customer
• Referrer’s account remains active
• Reward issued after the fraud/reversal window
Anti-abuse signals
• Repeated devices
• Repeated payment instruments
• Repeated addresses
• High cancellation patterns
• Circular referrals
• Unusual referral velocity
• Multiple accounts with the same identity evidence
These signals should trigger a review rather than automatic punishment. Collect only the
data required for fraud prevention and explain material account restrictions.

FIRST_BOOKING_CREATED
FIRST_SERVICE_COMPLETED
UNDER_REVIEW
QUALIFIED
REWARD_GRANTED
REVERSED
REJECTED

20. Promotional-credit rules
Every promotional offer should specify:
• Value
• Eligibility
• Minimum spend
• Applicable services
• Applicable city
• Applicable users
• Expiry date
• Whether it combines with other offers
• Maximum usage
• Refund treatment
Avoid vague copy such as:
₹500 added to your wallet!
Use:
₹500 promotional credit for eligible dog-walking bookings in Ahmedabad, valid until 31
August 2026.
21. Society credits
Society credits may come from:
• Society management sponsorship
• Resident launch campaign
• Event participation
• Corporate or RWA care benefit
• Resident subscription
They need an identifiable funding source.
society_credit_grant

### Page 430

- society_id
- customer_id
- programme_id
- funded_by
- amount
- validity
- eligible_services
The society dashboard should display aggregate utilisation, not individual resident pet-care
activity.
22. Loyalty system
Start with one simple loyalty mechanism:
Complete eligible services and earn Care Credits.
Example
• Earn one Care Point for every ₹100 of eligible platform spend
• 100 points = ₹100 promotional credit
• Points unlock only after service completion
• Refund reverses corresponding points
• Points expire after a clearly disclosed period
• No cash value
• No transfer
Better early rewards
Instead of only discounts, loyalty can offer:
• Priority booking
• Early access to peak slots
• Flexible rescheduling
• Grooming benefit
• Referral boost
• Society-event benefit

### Page 431

Avoid excessive discounting that damages unit economics.

Product 5: Pet Health Timeline
24. Product objective
The health timeline should make the app useful between bookings while improving care
continuity.
It should become:
A pet-parent-controlled record of care events, documents, observations and
reminders.
It should not become:
An automated veterinary diagnostic system.

### Page 432

25. Timeline categories
Category Example
Vaccination Rabies vaccination
Preventive care Deworming or tick treatment
Vet consultation Skin-allergy consultation
Medication Allergy medicine schedule
Grooming Full grooming completed
Weight 28 kg
Activity Monthly walking summary
Behaviour Pulling on leash
Training Recall improvement
Incident/observation Vomiting observed
Document Lab report or prescription
Reminder Vaccination due next month
Use factual language.
Prefer:
Vomiting observed twice during sitting service.
Avoid:
Gastroenteritis diagnosed.
unless a registered veterinarian supplied that diagnosis.
26. Timeline sources
Every timeline entry needs a visible source:
PET_PARENT

↓

↓

If AI extracts details from a document:
• Mark them as machine-extracted
• Show the source document

### Page 434

• Require owner confirmation
• Allow correction
• Preserve the original
• Do not treat extracted text as verified medical advice

30. Access control
Role Permitted access
Pet parent Complete record
Assigned sitter Current-service instructions only
Vet selected by owner Explicitly shared records
Groomer Relevant allergy/handling details
Customer support Minimal operational information
Marketing No access
Society/RWA No access
General city admin No routine clinical access
The DPDP Act and notified Rules require lawful-purpose processing, clear notices,
safeguards and mechanisms for exercising data rights. Pet records linked with an
identifiable owner, address or account should therefore be governed as part of the
customer’s personal-data environment.
31. Sharing records with a vet
Recommended flow:

### Page 437

Customer selects documents
↓
Customer sees recipient and purpose
↓
Time-limited access grant created
↓
Vet accesses selected records
↓
Access recorded in audit log
↓
Grant expires or customer revokes it
Do not give every partner vet access to every customer’s complete pet history.
32. Health reminders
The system can safely provide reminders such as:
• Vaccination record expires soon
• Customer-entered medicine reminder
• Grooming interval reached
• Follow-up date recorded in vet document
• Weight has not been updated recently
Wording should distinguish reminders from medical recommendations.
Use:
Bruno’s uploaded vaccination record shows the next date as 12 September. Confirm the
schedule with your veterinarian.
Avoid:
Bruno must receive this vaccine on 12 September.
33. Timeline-derived recommendations
Recommendations should use clear rules and transparent reasons.

### Page 438

Examples:
Grooming
Bruno’s last recorded grooming was 10 weeks ago. View available grooming services.
Training
Several walk reports mention leash pulling. Consider an introductory training consultation.
Vet follow-up
The uploaded consultation document includes a follow-up date of 25 August.
Senior care
Bella’s profile identifies her as a senior dog. Review senior-care support options.
These are service recommendations—not diagnosis.
34. Pet health timeline privacy controls
Customers should be able to:
• Add an event
• Correct an event
• Delete an owner-created event
• Download their records
• Revoke sharing
• View access history
• Set reminder preferences
• Request account/data deletion
• Control which carers see specific instructions
Separate:
• Essential current-service instructions
• Historical records
• Veterinary documents
• Marketing recommendations
A sitter should not automatically receive a pet’s entire medical history because they are
assigned one walk.

### Page 439

35. Pet health timeline KPIs
Metric Purpose
Profiles with timeline events Adoption
Vaccination documents uploaded Utility
Reminders created Engagement
Reminder completion Usefulness
Records shared with vets Coordination value
Owner corrections Extraction quality
Access revocations Privacy control
Grooming reminder conversion Commercial value
Training-consult conversion Cross-sell
Support/privacy complaints Trust risk
Avoid optimising solely for:
• Number of health alerts
• Number of veterinary bookings
• Number of medical-related notifications
Too many reminders can create anxiety and destroy trust.
36. Recommended implementation order
Wave 1: Matching foundation

• Rules-based top-three ranking

• Override logging
• Outcome tracking

Wave 3: Loyalty
• Care Points
• Non-cash benefits
• Referral tiers
• Contribution monitoring
Wave 4: Health timeline MVP
• Manual timeline entries
• Document uploads
• Reminders

Wave 5: Advanced intelligence
• Calibrated prediction models
• Matching optimisation
• Document extraction
• Personalised service recommendations
• Health-timeline summaries
Final approval matrix
Proposal Final decision

Proposal Final decision

Phase 11 product doctrine
AI should improve the administrator’s decision—not hide the decision. Credits should
reward repeat care—not become an unregulated cash substitute. Pet records should
improve continuity—not become an automated medical authority.
Win condition
The three systems succeed when:
• Admin assignment time decreases
• Matching quality and repeat relationships improve

### Page 442

• Sitter opportunities remain explainable and auditable
• Credits increase profitable repeat usage
• Referral acquisition remains fraud-controlled
• Pet parents maintain useful care histories
• Medical, payment and privacy boundaries remain intact
AI Matching, Loyalty Credits and Pet Health Timeline: GO—with human approval,
immutable ledgers, transparent scoring and owner-controlled records.
Phase 11 Advanced Command Center and B2B Dashboards

Approve all four product directions, with several safeguards:

Advanced admin command
Approve—highest priority with sitter app
center

Approve as support-assistance, not pressure
Subscription churn intelligence
automation
Society web dashboard Approve; do not build a native app yet
Partner web dashboard Approve; use role-specific modules
The command center should not become a screen full of charts. It should be an exception-
management system:
Operational events
↓

↓

Product 3: Advanced Admin Command Center
1. Core objective
The command center should help the team answer five questions immediately:
1. What is happening now?
2. What is likely to fail?
3. Who must act?
4. How long do they have?
5. Was the issue resolved correctly?
It should combine:
• Live operations
• Booking-risk management
• Safety and incidents
• Supply and capacity
• Subscription retention
• Customer support
• City and financial performance
Use consistent application events, metrics, traces and logs rather than building every
dashboard from unrelated manual spreadsheets. OpenTelemetry’s observability model
similarly separates telemetry into traces, metrics and logs so teams can understand
system behaviour from multiple connected signals.
2. Command-center information architecture
National command center
Used by:
• Founders
• National operations
• Safety team

### Page 444

• Finance
• Central support
• Expansion leaders
Main sections
Network Overview
Cities
Live Operations
Bookings at Risk
Safety and Incidents
Sitters and Partners
Subscriptions
Customer Support
Capacity
Finance
Societies
System Health
City command center
Used by:
• City manager
• Operations coordinator
• Local support
• Supply manager
• Society executive
It should automatically scope data to the staff member’s assigned city and zones.
PostgreSQL row-level security can restrict which rows a role may read, insert, update or
delete. It is appropriate as a defence-in-depth layer for isolating city, society, booking and
incident data, alongside application-level authorisation.
3. Live operations map

### Page 445

Purpose
Display operationally relevant live services—not the continuous location of every sitter.
Show
• Services in progress
• Sitters en route to accepted bookings
• Late arrivals
• Tracking interruptions
• Backup assignments
• Active incidents
• Unassigned urgent bookings
Do not show
• Sitters who are off duty
• Sitters outside an active or imminent booking
• Historical home locations
• Location to marketing or society users
• Precise routes after the operational retention period
Map states
State Meaning
Healthy Service progressing normally
Watch Minor risk signal
Action required Operations intervention needed
Critical Safety or fulfilment escalation
Offline Live data temporarily unavailable
Completed Service finished
Example card
Bruno · Dog Walk
Sitter: Riya
City: Ahmedabad

### Page 446

Status: In progress
Started: 7:34 AM
Tracking: Healthy
Elapsed: 22 min
Risk: Low
[Open Booking] [Contact] [View Route]
Location, addresses, schedules and incident information should be access-limited,
purpose-specific and auditable under the Indian digital-data protection framework. The
notified DPDP Rules, 2025 have phased commencement, but the product should
implement those controls before expanding access across cities and partner roles.
4. City health score
A city health score can be useful, but one number must never hide a safety failure.
Recommended score
Dimension Weight
Safety and quality 30%
Fulfilment and supply 20%
Retention and demand 20%
Economics 20%
Support and compliance 10%
Example
Ahmedabad City Health: 87/100 · Approved
Safety: 28/30
Fulfilment: 18/20
Retention: 16/20
Economics: 17/20
Support: 8/10

### Page 447

Hard overrides
The city cannot be labelled “healthy” when any of these is true:
• Unresolved critical incident
• Partner payouts materially overdue
• Critical documents expired
• Cross-city data exposure
• Booking completion below minimum
• Finance unreconciled
• Safety incidents deliberately misclassified
Recommended health bands
Score Status Action
90–100 Excellent Eligible for controlled growth
80–89 Healthy Normal operation
70–79 Monitor Restrict expansion
60–69 Intervention Freeze new clusters
Below 60 Critical Consider service/city pause
5. Sitter risk list
Rename this module:
Sitter Quality Review Queue
“Risk list” can become an unfair blacklist if it combines weak, noisy or outdated signals.
Signals
• Repeated late arrivals
• Sitter-initiated cancellations
• No-shows
• Missing reports
• Unexplained GPS interruptions
• Low recent ratings

### Page 448

• Incident involvement
• Expired documents
• Unresolved payout dispute
• Abnormal workload
• Repeated customer complaints
Context required
Each signal should show:
• Severity
• Frequency
• Date
• Service volume denominator
• Whether the concern was verified
• Whether the sitter appealed
• Corrective training completed
• Current review status
A sitter with one complaint across 500 bookings should not be treated the same as a sitter
with three complaints across ten bookings.
Review states
NORMAL
WATCH
COACHING_REQUIRED
BOOKING_LIMITED
TEMPORARILY_PAUSED
INVESTIGATION
REINSTATED
REMOVED
Required protections
• Clear reasons
• Human review

### Page 449

• Appeal/dispute mechanism
• No automatic lifetime penalty
• Separate verified incidents from allegations
• Exclude customer/admin-caused failures
• Model version and audit history
6. Delayed-service alerts
A delayed-service alert should create an actionable operational queue.
Alert progression
Booking approaching start
↓
Sitter not en route
↓
Sitter reminder
↓
Grace period exceeded
↓
Operations notified
↓
Backup checked
↓
Customer proactively updated
Alert record
• Booking
• Scheduled time
• Current sitter
• Latest acknowledgement
• Travel estimate
• Customer communication status

### Page 450

• Backup availability
• Assigned operations owner
• SLA deadline
Prometheus guidance recommends alerting on actionable symptoms and avoiding alerts
where there is no meaningful response. The command center should therefore suppress
duplicates, group related alerts and always present a next action.
7. Incident heatmap
The heatmap should help identify recurring operational causes—not publicly label
neighbourhoods or sitters as dangerous.
Dimensions
• City and service zone
• Society
• Service type
• Time of day
• Incident category
• Pet-risk classification
• Provider-experience level
• Root cause
• Severity
Incident categories
Category Examples
Access Gate denial, wrong address
Pet handling Escape risk, aggression
Medical Pet distress, injury
Scheduling No-show, late arrival
Customer handover Customer unavailable
Technology Tracking/app failure

### Page 451

Category Examples
Partner quality Hygiene or service concern
Security/privacy Wrong data or access exposure
Heatmap safeguards
• Minimum sample-size threshold
• No personally identifying labels
• Separate verified incidents from reports under investigation
• Show exposure rate, not just raw counts
• Compare against booking volume
• Retain drill-down only for authorised safety roles
Example:
Wrong:
Powai has 15 incidents.
Better:
Powai had 15 reportable events across 4,200 services:
0 critical, 2 serious and 13 minor.
8. Revenue by city and service
The financial module must distinguish:
Metric Meaning
GMV Total customer spending

City operating contribution Contribution minus local fixed costs
Fully allocated result After central cost allocation

### Page 452

Required views
• City

9. Capacity planning module
Purpose
Prevent the company from selling services or subscriptions that the city cannot deliver.
Show by city, zone and time window
• Gross provider capacity
• Existing booking commitments
• Subscription reservations
• Backup reserve
• Leave impact
• Remaining sellable capacity
• Stop-sales threshold
Capacity statuses
Utilisation Status
Below 70% Healthy

### Page 453

Utilisation Status
No backup Block high-frequency plans

• Pause a time slot
• Pause a plan
• Move demand to another window
• Recruit providers
• Increase backup reserve
• Waitlist customers
• Open an adjacent zone only after gate approval

Queues
• General questions

Metrics

### Page 454

Metric Purpose

Severity Example Target
P0 Pet missing/serious danger Immediate

P2 Same-day booking/payment Defined short SLA
P3 General query Business-hour SLA
Booking Risk Score
11. Product objective
Identify bookings that require additional preparation before they fail.
The score should:
• Be calculated before confirmation
• Update when conditions change
• Explain every risk factor
• Recommend actions
• Remain reviewable by humans
NIST’s AI Risk Management Framework recommends documenting human oversight,
system limitations, explanations and monitoring of unexpected behaviour or performance
degradation.
12. Corrected risk factors

### Page 455

Factor Recommended treatment
First-time customer Low–medium context signal
First booking for pet Medium
New sitter Medium; do not automatically penalise heavily
High pet-risk classification High
Long travel time High
No approved backup High/critical for subscriptions
Peak-time booking Medium
Unpaid booking Block confirmation rather than score it
Previous complaint Depends on severity, recency and resolution
Boarding Higher custody exposure
Medication/senior care High or specialist review
Society access incomplete High
Tight back-to-back schedule High
Expired sitter document Ineligible, not merely risky
Open sitter investigation Ineligible or senior review
Important correction
Some conditions should be hard blockers, not score factors:
Payment not verified
Required document expired
Sitter not qualified
No service capacity
Sitter under suspension
Service unavailable in zone
A 95/100 match must not override an expired document or failed payment.
13. Booking-risk score design

### Page 456

Use an explainable point score first.
Example
Factor Points
First booking for pet +8
New sitter +8
Yellow pet-risk level +15
Red/specialist pet +30
Travel above target +15
No backup +20
Peak-time slot +8
Prior unresolved complaint +20
Boarding service +15
Medication required +20
Gate approval missing +15
Mitigating factors
Mitigation Adjustment
Previous successful pet-sitter relationship −20

### Page 459

Subscription Intelligence
17. Churn-risk objective
Identify subscribers who may need product or service recovery before renewal.
The goal is not:
Find users who can be pressured into paying.
The goal is:
Detect service-plan mismatch and offer a relevant remedy.
18. Corrected churn signals
Signal Likely interpretation
Unused credits Plan may be oversized
Credits exhausted early Upgrade opportunity
Low service rating Quality problem
Repeated sitter changes Continuity problem
Payment failure Payment-method issue
Repeated missed walks Timing mismatch
Multiple support complaints Unresolved dissatisfaction
Repeated pauses Seasonal or poor plan fit
Drop in app/report usage Lower engagement
Cancellation-page visit Strong churn intent
Preferred sitter unavailable Trust risk
Travel dates Temporary pause opportunity
Do not use sensitive pet-health information for marketing or churn pressure without a
clearly justified purpose and suitable customer controls.
19. Churn-risk model

### Page 460

Begin with rule-based scoring:
Signal Example points

Payment failure +25

Preferred sitter unavailable +15
Customer opened cancellation flow +30

Score Level Recommended action
0–19 Healthy No intervention
20–39 Watch In-app education
40–59 High Support review
60+ Critical Human retention outreach
20. Remedy engine
Do not immediately offer discounts.

Churn cause Best response
Low utilisation Downgrade or pause

Sitter changes Primary/backup review

Phase 11 command-center doctrine
Predict only what can be explained, alert only when somebody can act, expose only
the data a role needs and keep humans accountable for high-impact decisions.
Win condition

### Page 474

The system succeeds when:
• Operations intervene before bookings fail
• Safety alerts are faster and less noisy
• City performance is directly comparable
• Subscription remedies match actual churn causes
• Societies receive useful aggregate reports without resident surveillance
• Partners manage bookings and payouts independently
• Every override and sensitive action remains auditable
Advanced Admin Command Center: GO—with event-driven operations, explainable
risk scores, human review and strict role-based data access.
Phase 11 Database, API, Security and Location-Privacy Architecture
Executive decision
Your proposed foundation is good, but it needs several production-grade corrections:
1. Separate devices, push tokens and authenticated sessions.
2. Separate tracking sessions, GPS points and operational events.
3. Store matching recommendations as versioned runs with explanations and
admin decisions.
4. Replace the mutable wallet.balance model with an immutable service-credit
ledger.
5. Split pet timeline events, documents, reminders and sharing permissions.
6. Make all APIs object-authorized, idempotent and role-scoped.
7. Treat live location as a temporary, purpose-limited operational data stream.
The corrected architecture should be:
Mobile device
├── authenticated session
├── push token
└── app/version state
Booking

### Page 475

├── service session
├── tracking session
├── tracking points
├── operational events
├── service report
└── incidents
Customer
├── credit accounts
├── immutable credit ledger
├── sitter preferences
└── pet timeline
1. Device and push-token schema
Problem with the current table
device_tokens
- user_id
- device_type
- token
- app_version
This combines three different concepts:
• The physical/app device installation
• The push-notification token
• The authenticated user session
One user may have several devices, multiple users may sequentially use the same device,
and push tokens can rotate or become invalid. Firebase recommends storing a timestamp
with registration tokens, refreshing token records periodically and removing tokens that are
stale or rejected as invalid.
Recommended tables

### Page 476

mobile_devices
- id
- user_id nullable
- installation_id
- app_type // CUSTOMER, SITTER
- platform // IOS, ANDROID
- device_model
- os_version
- app_version
- build_number
- locale
- timezone
- status
- first_seen_at
- last_seen_at
- created_at
- updated_at
push_tokens
- id
- device_id
- provider // FCM, APNS, EXPO
- token_encrypted
- environment // DEVELOPMENT, PRODUCTION
- status // ACTIVE, INVALID, REVOKED
- notifications_enabled
- registered_at
- last_validated_at
- last_sent_at
- invalidated_at

### Page 477

- created_at
- updated_at
mobile_sessions
- id
- user_id
- device_id
- refresh_token_family_id
- refresh_token_hash
- status
- issued_at
- last_used_at
- expires_at
- revoked_at
- compromise_detected_at
- last_ip_hash
- created_at
Important rules
• Use a stable app-generated installation_id.
• Keep one push-token record per app installation and provider.
• Never treat a push token as an authentication credential.
• Encrypt the usable token at rest because the server must retrieve it to send
notifications.
• Do not write push tokens into application logs.
• Mark tokens invalid after permanent provider errors.
• Detach or revoke the token when a user logs out of a shared device.
Refresh tokens should use rotation: each refresh issues a new refresh token and
invalidates the previous one while retaining the token-family relationship for replay
detection. This is the current OAuth security best practice.

### Page 478

On the device, refresh tokens should be stored in secure platform-backed storage, such as
Expo SecureStore, rather than AsyncStorage or ordinary local files. Expo SecureStore is
intended for small secrets such as tokens and keys.

This table mixes:
• High-frequency GPS samples

and access requirements.

GPS points

### Page 480

- created_at
PostGIS supports geography(Point,4326) for longitude/latitude coordinates and GiST
spatial indexes, which is preferable to repeatedly processing separate unindexed lat and
lng values.
For high-volume production use:
• Partition tracking_points by time once volume justifies it.
• Index tracking_session_id, recorded_at_device.
• Add a GiST index on location.
• Batch-upload points rather than making one HTTP request per point.
• Use sequence_number and idempotency keys to recover offline data safely.
• Store raw tracking data separately from derived distance and route summaries.

Scoring rule
Use:
Riya — Match score 92/100
Do not label it “92% probability” unless the model is statistically calibrated and validated
as a probability.
Hard eligibility filters must execute before ranking:
• Current verification
• Valid documents
• Correct service permission
• Availability
• Pet-risk compatibility
• Capacity

• Service-zone access
• No suspension
• No schedule conflict

4. Replace the wallet with a credit ledger
Problem with this schema
wallets
- user_id
- balance
A mutable balance does not provide a reliable audit trail and can become inconsistent
during concurrent bookings, refunds and retries.
There is also a regulatory distinction between internal service credits and a cash-like
prepaid wallet. RBI states that a closed-system instrument can only purchase the issuer’s
own goods or services, cannot support cash withdrawal and cannot settle third-party
services. A marketplace involving independent service providers should therefore obtain
payments advice before creating reloadable cash balances.
Recommended early model
Name the feature Credits, not Wallet.
credit_accounts
- id
- customer_id
- credit_type
- currency
- status
- created_at
- updated_at

SUBSCRIPTION
REFERRAL

SERVICE_RECOVERY

Balance calculation

### Page 486

Available credits
=
grants
+ releases
+ restores
− reservations
− consumption
− reversals
− expiry
A cached available_balance_minor may be maintained for performance, but the
immutable ledger remains the financial source of truth.
Controls
• Store money in minor units, such as paise.
• Require idempotency keys for every adjustment.
• Every admin adjustment needs a reason.
• Require dual approval above a configured threshold.
• Do not allow customer transfers or cash withdrawal.
• Do not force eligible cash refunds into platform credits without customer choice.
