8. Corrected mobile authentication APIs
Use a versioned API namespace.
POST /v1/mobile/auth/login
POST /v1/mobile/auth/refresh
POST /v1/mobile/auth/logout
GET /v1/mobile/auth/sessions
DELETE /v1/mobile/auth/sessions/{sessionId}
Login response
{
"access_token": "...",
"access_token_expires_at": "...",
"refresh_token": "...",
"session_id": "...",
"device_id": "..."
}
Security rules
• Short-lived access token
• Rotating, per-device refresh token

### Page 492

• Refresh-token reuse detection
• Server-side session revocation
• Rate limiting and OTP abuse protection
• Logout from one device or every device
• MFA for national and city administrators
• Separate mobile and admin session policies
OWASP identifies broken authentication and broken object-level authorization as major
API risks. Every endpoint accepting an object identifier must verify that the authenticated
actor is authorized to access that particular object.
9. Device-token APIs
Replace generic token endpoints with device-scoped endpoints:
PUT /v1/mobile/devices/{deviceId}/push-token
DELETE /v1/mobile/devices/{deviceId}/push-token
PATCH /v1/mobile/devices/{deviceId}
Example registration:
{
"provider": "FCM",
"token": "...",
"app_type": "SITTER",
"platform": "ANDROID",
"environment": "PRODUCTION",
"app_version": "2.3.0",
"build_number": "147"
}
The deviceId must belong to the current authenticated session.
10. Push-notification APIs
Do not expose this publicly

### Page 493

POST /api/notifications/push
A general client-callable push endpoint could be abused to send arbitrary notifications.
Use an internal event-driven service:
POST /internal/v1/notifications/events
POST /internal/v1/notifications/send
Only trusted backend services or tightly authorized admins should invoke it.
Customer-facing APIs:
GET /v1/me/notification-preferences
PATCH /v1/me/notification-preferences
GET /v1/me/notifications
POST /v1/me/notifications/{notificationId}/read
Store critical booking state in the backend and in-app inbox. Push is a delivery mechanism,
not proof that the user received or read an operational update.
11. Advanced tracking APIs
Corrected endpoints
POST /v1/sitter/bookings/{bookingId}/tracking-sessions
POST /v1/tracking-sessions/{sessionId}/points:batch
POST /v1/tracking-sessions/{sessionId}/events
POST /v1/tracking-sessions/{sessionId}/end
GET /v1/customer/bookings/{bookingId}/live-tracking
GET /v1/customer/bookings/{bookingId}/route-replay
GET /v1/admin/bookings/{bookingId}/tracking
Why batch points?
Instead of:
POST /api/tracking/point
for every coordinate, send small batches:
{

### Page 494

"batch_id": "uuid",
"points": [
{
"sequence": 101,
"recorded_at": "...",
"latitude": 23.01,
"longitude": 72.52,
"accuracy_meters": 12
}
]
}
This is more resilient to weak connectivity and lowers network and battery overhead.
Required controls
• The booking must be assigned to the sitter.
• The service must permit tracking.
• Only one active session is allowed per booking.
• Start and end calls require idempotency keys.
• Points outside the active session are rejected.
• Customer access is limited to their booking.
• Admin access is limited to operations/safety roles.
• Route replay omits pre-service and post-service travel.
Expo supports location subscriptions and background-task integration, but background
execution remains subject to mobile-platform restrictions, so the backend must explicitly
represent interrupted or incomplete tracking rather than fabricating a continuous route.
12. Matching APIs
GET /v1/admin/bookings/{bookingId}/matching-recommendations
POST /v1/admin/bookings/{bookingId}/matching-recommendations:refresh
POST /v1/admin/bookings/{bookingId}/assignments

### Page 495

Assignment payload:
{
"matching_run_id": "uuid",
"sitter_id": "uuid",
"candidate_id": "uuid",
"decision_type": "RECOMMENDED",
"override_reason": null,
"expected_booking_version": 14
}
Use optimistic concurrency through expected_booking_version so that two admins cannot
assign different sitters simultaneously.
13. Credit APIs
Read APIs
GET /v1/me/credits
GET /v1/me/credit-transactions
Do not use a separate client-controlled apply endpoint
POST /api/wallet/apply-credit
Credit reservation and use should be part of the atomic booking checkout:
POST /v1/bookings/quote
POST /v1/bookings
Example:
{
"pet_id": "...",
"service_package_id": "...",
"scheduled_at": "...",
"payment_method": "SERVICE_CREDITS",
"credit_account_id": "...",
"idempotency_key": "..."

### Page 496

}
The backend must:
1. Lock or safely reserve the available credit.
2. Create the booking.
3. Record the ledger reservation.
4. Roll back the entire operation if any step fails.
Admin API:
POST /v1/admin/customers/{customerId}/credit-adjustments
Required fields:
• Credit type
• Amount
• Reason code
• Case or booking reference
• Idempotency key
• Approver where required

GET /v1/pets/{petId}/health-timeline
POST /v1/pets/{petId}/health-timeline
PATCH /v1/pets/{petId}/health-timeline/{eventId}
DELETE /v1/pets/{petId}/health-timeline/{eventId}
Your proposed delete endpoint contains a typo:
:eventide
It should be:
:eventId
Additional endpoints:
POST /v1/pets/{petId}/health-documents/upload-url
POST /v1/pets/{petId}/health-reminders
PATCH /v1/pets/{petId}/health-reminders/{reminderId}

POST /v1/pets/{petId}/record-access-grants
DELETE /v1/pets/{petId}/record-access-grants/{grantId}
GET /v1/pets/{petId}/record-access-log
An update should usually use PATCH, because clients commonly change only selected
fields.
15. Feedback API
Add the missing endpoint:
POST /v1/mobile/feedback
GET /v1/admin/mobile-feedback
PATCH /v1/admin/mobile-feedback/{feedbackId}
The mobile client should send only explicitly allowlisted diagnostic fields.
16. Security requirements
Authentication
• Rotating refresh tokens
• Short-lived access tokens
• Per-device sessions
• Session revocation
• OTP and login rate limits
• MFA for administrators
• Reauthentication for high-risk actions
• No tokens in URLs or logs
Authorization
Use RBAC plus contextual checks:
Role
+ city scope
+ service-zone scope
+ booking relationship

### Page 498

+ action permission
Examples:
• A sitter can read only currently assigned bookings.
• A customer can read only their own pets and bookings.
• A city manager can access only assigned cities.
• A society user can see aggregate society information.
• A vet can access only explicitly shared pet records.
PostgreSQL Row-Level Security can restrict which rows a database role may select or
modify and provides a default-deny posture when enabled without an applicable policy.
Use it as defence in depth, not as a replacement for application authorization.
Device integrity
Consider Play Integrity for high-risk Android actions such as:
• Login from a new device
• Credit adjustment attempts
• Suspicious tracking submissions
• Referral qualification
• High-value booking activity
Play Integrity allows the backend to assess whether requests originate from the genuine
app installed through Google Play on a recognized device. It should provide a risk signal—
not automatically block every unsupported device.
Secure local storage
Store in SecureStore or platform key storage:
• Refresh token
• Device-session identifier
• Small encryption keys
Do not store there:
• Large pet records
• GPS history
• Media
• Complete booking databases

### Page 499

Media uploads
OWASP recommends allowlisting file types, validating content rather than trusting the
filename or HTTP content type, limiting sizes, generating safe filenames, storing files
outside executable web roots and applying malware scanning where appropriate.
Required controls:
• Signed uploads
• MIME and magic-byte validation
• Size and duration limits
• Image/video transcoding
• Malware scanning
• Randomized storage keys
• No public buckets for private records
• Expiring download URLs
• Metadata stripping where appropriate
Logging
Never log:
• Refresh/access tokens
• Push tokens
• Gate codes
• Exact health documents
• Full GPS payloads
• Payment data
• Customer access instructions
Log:
• Actor
• Action
• Target entity
• Result
• Timestamp

### Page 500

• City
• Device/session ID
• Correlation ID
• Security reason
Forced updates
Support:
minimum_supported_version
recommended_version
update_mode
Modes:
OPTIONAL
RECOMMENDED
REQUIRED
SECURITY_BLOCK
Do not force an update during an active walk unless the existing app version presents an
immediate security or safety risk. Allow completion of the live service, then block further
bookings.
17. Location privacy policy
Mandatory rules
1. Track only during a sitter-initiated eligible service.
2. Stop tracking automatically when the service ends.
3. Do not track during off-duty periods.
4. Explain foreground and background use before requesting permission.
5. Show tracking status visibly to the sitter.
6. Give customers access only to their active or historical booking.
7. Restrict admin access to authorized operations and safety roles.
8. Log every sensitive administrative access.
9. Define and enforce route-data retention.

### Page 501

10. Delete or de-identify raw points after the retention purpose expires.
11. Retain incident-related routes separately under a documented legal or investigation
hold.
12. Do not send raw routes to general analytics tools.
Android requires developers to accurately declare location-data collection and sharing,
and background-location access is treated distinctly from ordinary foreground location.
The DPDP Act requires consent requests to be presented clearly and in plain language,
while the notified Rules establish the current implementation framework. Location notices
should therefore explain the purpose, duration, recipients, controls and grievance contact
in straightforward language.
Recommended data separation
Exact GPS points
→ restricted tracking store
Derived distance/duration
→ ordinary booking report
Service zone and city
→ operational analytics
De-identified aggregate patterns
→ capacity and route analysis
Exact customer coordinates and access codes should not be available to marketing,
finance or society roles.
18. User-facing location wording
Short permission explanation
Location during active services
Your location is collected only while you start and perform an active pet-care service. It
helps verify arrival, share live service updates, document the service route and provide
safety support. Tracking stops when the service ends.

### Page 502

Detailed wording
When you start an eligible pet-care service, the app may collect your location while the app
is open and, where required for route continuity, while it runs in the background. The pet
parent can view location updates only for their active booking. Authorized operations and
safety staff may access the information when needed to manage the service, investigate an
incident or provide support.
The app does not track your location outside an active service. Route data is retained only
for the period described in our privacy notice and is then deleted or de-identified unless it
is required for an open safety, support or legal matter.
Interrupted tracking message for customers
Live location is temporarily unavailable. The service remains in progress, and our
operations team has been notified.
Avoid blaming the sitter when the cause may be:
• Network loss
• Permission change
• Device shutdown
• Operating-system restriction
• GPS inaccuracy

Phase 11 technical win condition
Every mobile action is attributable to a device and session, every service has a
defensible event history, every credit change is auditable, every record is role-scoped
and location is collected only for a clearly defined active-service purpose.
Phase 11 database, API, security and privacy architecture: GO—with the corrected
normalized schemas, immutable ledgers, rotating sessions and purpose-limited
tracking model above
Phase 11 Six-Month Execution Plan
Executive verdict
Approve the six-month roadmap, with five structural corrections:

### Page 504

1. Prototype background GPS in Month 1, rather than discovering technical
limitations in Week 9.
2. Build the admin command centre alongside the sitter app, because it must
receive and act on sitter events.
3. Replace “wallet” with an immutable service-credit ledger. Defer customer-funded
cash balances.
4. Launch rules-based, explainable matching first; advanced AI comes after
sufficient outcome data exists.
5. Security, privacy, physical-device QA and app-store preparation must run
throughout the programme—not only during Week 23.
The corrected delivery sequence is:
Product and technical validation
↓
Sitter app foundation
↓
Tracking + emergency + admin operations
↓
Customer app
↓
Subscriptions + credits + referrals
↓
Matching assistance + hardening
↓
Controlled store launch
Expo background location and remote push notifications require development builds
rather than relying only on Expo Go. Expo also documents that background location stops if
the user terminates the application, so this limitation must be tested and represented
truthfully in the operational state model from the first month.

Phase 11 six-month win condition
The sitter app reliably captures and proves service execution, the command centre
surfaces failures before they affect customers, and the customer app increases self-
service and retention—without introducing unsafe tracking, credit errors or
uncontrolled operational complexity.
Final Phase 11 execution decision: GO—with GPS validation moved forward, admin
operations developed in parallel, service credits replacing the cash wallet, and staged
store release after continuous QA and security verification

Description and claims
Customer app positioning
Book verified local pet-care services, follow active service updates, receive care reports
and manage your recurring pet-care plans.
Sitter app positioning
Manage assigned pet-care bookings, document services, submit care reports, access
safety support and track approved earnings.

• 100% safe
• Guaranteed sitter
• Always accurate GPS
• Instant veterinary emergency response
• Best pet-care app
• Government-approved sitters
Claims should correspond to measurable, available functionality.

Do not provide reviewers with access to real customer or sitter records.
3. Permission strategy
Main principle
Request a permission at the moment the user activates the related feature—not during first
launch.
Android recommends associating runtime permission requests with the specific user
action requiring them. Google Play also restricts sensitive permissions to current,
disclosed and genuinely necessary features.

### Page 553

Location
Sitter-app use
Location is required for:
• Arrival confirmation
• Active-walk tracking
• Route proof
• Safety support
• Customer active-service updates
Rules
• Request when the sitter prepares for the first tracked service
• Track only between service start and end
• Show an active tracking indicator
• Stop automatically after completion
• Store routes for a documented period
• Do not use routes for marketing
• Do not track general sitter availability
• Allow operations access only when required
Google Play requires background location to be core to the app, visibly disclosed in-app,
documented in the privacy policy and supported by a permissions declaration and
demonstration for review.
Recommended sitter disclosure
Location during active services
The app uses your location only while you perform an eligible pet-care service. This verifies
arrival, provides service-route updates and supports safety assistance. Tracking stops
when the service is completed.
Customer-app location
The customer app usually does not require continuous location permission.
Use:
• Address entry
• Map pin

### Page 554

• Optional one-time current-location selection
Do not request persistent customer location merely to make booking slightly faster.
Camera
Use for:
• Pet photos
• Service-proof images
• Report cards
• Incident evidence
• Health-document capture
Recommended disclosure
Camera access lets you capture pet-care updates, service reports and documents when
you choose.
Request permission only when the user taps:
• Take photo
• Record update
• Scan document
Photo library
Use for:
• Uploading existing pet photos
• Selecting vaccination records
• Adding service-proof media
• Uploading profile images
Prefer a system media picker and limited selection rather than asking for unrestricted
library access where platform capabilities permit it.
Disclosure
Photo access lets you select specific pet photos and care documents to upload.

### Page 555

Notifications
Operational notifications include:
• Booking request
• Assignment
• Sitter en route
• Service started
• Report available
• Payment failed
• Renewal due
• Emergency update
Ask after explaining the value:
Enable notifications for booking assignments, service updates, reports and urgent support
messages.
Separate:
• Operational notifications
• Subscription reminders
• Product announcements
• Promotions
A user who disables marketing should still be able to receive necessary service
communications through permitted channels.
Microphone and video
Keep these optional.
Use for:
• Short pet-care video update
• Incident evidence
• Optional sitter introduction
Do not request microphone access for ordinary photo capture.
Disclosure

### Page 556

Microphone access is used only when you record a video or audio update.
Request the permission only after the user explicitly starts recording.

Mistake 6: Treating store approval as a final-week task
Background location, privacy disclosures, demo accounts, screenshots and reviewer
instructions need preparation before submission.
Google Play’s background-location review can require:
• Permission declaration
• Prominent disclosure
• Privacy policy
• Video demonstration
• A store listing that visibly describes the core location feature.
Mistake 7: Removing the PWA immediately
The PWA should remain available for:
• Customers who do not install apps
• Unsupported devices
• Booking during store outages
• New customers
• Emergency fallbacks
• Search acquisition
• Society-specific links
The app is an additional high-retention channel—not the only access channel.

Phase 11 launch doctrine
Release to the smallest group that can prove the next level of product reliability, then
expand only after the data shows that service quality, privacy and operations remain
stable.
Phase 11 launch win condition
Sitters execute most services through the mobile workflow, customers receive
reliable live visibility and self-service controls, and each new city can activate the
apps without increasing incidents, support burden or operational failure.
Final app-launch decision: GO—with progressive cohorts, city-level feature flags,
contextual permissions, complete store disclosures and no forced migration from the
web platform.

Risk 1: App development delays
Root causes
• Building sitter and customer apps simultaneously
• Adding AI, loyalty and health features before the core workflow works
• Discovering GPS limitations late

### Page 571

• Rebuilding backend logic inside the app
• Treating every feature as P0
Prevention
Use four controlled release units:
1. Sitter service-execution vertical slice
2. Admin exception-management workflow
3. Customer booking and live-status workflow
4. Retention and intelligence features
The first production-quality vertical slice should cover:
Login
→ Assigned booking
→ Arrival
→ Start service
→ GPS/session evidence
→ End service
→ Report submission
→ Admin visibility
Do not begin customer loyalty or advanced AI while this flow remains unreliable.
Leading indicators
• Increasing unfinished work each sprint
• More than 20–25% of sprint work carried forward
• Backend contracts changing after mobile development begins
• Native-location prototype still unproven after Month 1
• P0 defect count growing rather than shrinking
Risk 2: GPS battery drain and unreliable tracking
A single aggressive location interval is not appropriate for every service. Use adaptive
collection based on movement, accuracy, battery condition and service type.

### Page 572

Expo supports background-location tasks through development builds, but its
documentation notes that updates stop if the user terminates the app and resume when
the app is reopened. The product must represent this as an interruption rather than
showing a falsely continuous route.
Required controls
Control Requirement
Tracking period Active eligible service only
Update strategy Time- and distance-adaptive
Offline behaviour Queue points locally
End behaviour Stop automatically
App terminated Mark tracking interrupted
Customer map Display freshness and quality
Sitting/grooming Arrival and check-ins, not continuous routes
Battery monitoring Test real devices and OEMs
Stop condition
Pause tracking rollout when:
• Tracking continues after service completion
• Battery or thermal issues materially affect devices
• More than 5% of eligible sessions fail without a recovery path
• Location points are silently lost
• Staff cannot distinguish network loss from deliberate tracking disablement
Risk 3: Privacy concerns
The official rule must be:
Location is collected only during an explicitly started active pet-care service and is
stopped when that service ends.
Google Play requires background-location access to support an important core feature,
accompanied by prominent in-app disclosure, a privacy policy and an accurate
permissions declaration. Apple also requires location collection to be directly relevant to
app functionality and accurately disclosed.

### Page 573

The Indian DPDP framework requires digital personal data to be processed for lawful
purposes with clear notices and appropriate safeguards. The 2025 Rules use phased
commencement, so product controls should be built before wider enforcement and scale.
Mandatory safeguards
• No off-duty or between-booking tracking
• Visible tracking indicator
• Customer access only to their booking
• Admin access limited to operations and safety roles
• Access audit history
• Defined raw-route retention
• Incident-related legal hold handled separately
• Raw routes excluded from ordinary analytics
• Sitter can see permission and tracking status
• Privacy notice states purpose, access and retention
Approved user-facing wording
Location during active services
Your location is collected only while you perform an eligible pet-care service. It helps verify
arrival, provide service updates, document the route and support safety assistance.
Tracking stops when the service is completed.
Risk 4: Bugs during real bookings
A mobile defect can cause more than inconvenience. It can affect pet custody, customer
access, payment, provider earnings and incident handling.
Required protections
Internal alpha
→ Sitter closed beta
→ Customer closed beta
→ One-cluster production
→ One-city production
→ Multi-city rollout
Use independent feature flags for:

### Page 574

• Mobile booking
• GPS
• Route sharing
• Credit payments
• AI recommendations
• Emergency workflows
• City and service-zone availability
Keep the PWA operational as a fallback.
Apple TestFlight supports up to 100 internal testers and up to 10,000 external testers.
Google recommends moving from internal testing to a controlled closed test; certain newer
personal Play developer accounts must complete a closed test with at least 12 testers
opted in for 14 continuous days before applying for production access.
Release blockers
• Duplicate service start or completion
• Cross-customer access
• Incorrect credit consumption
• Incorrect payout calculation
• Unrecoverable offline report
• Emergency alert not delivered to the backend
• Tracking outside active service
• Crash during the main service flow
• Missing account-deletion path
Risk 5: Low sitter adoption
The sitter app will fail if it feels like surveillance, adds unnecessary steps or works poorly on
common Android devices.
Adoption strategy
Stage Approach
First 5–10 sitters Guided pilot and direct observation

### Page 575

Stage Approach
20–50 sitters Training plus structured feedback
One city App becomes default, with support
Stable production App required for eligible services
Product principles
• Three-tap access to the next booking
• Large Start, Emergency and Complete actions
• Offline support
• Earnings transparency
• Clear tracking status
• No hidden scoring
• Training in local languages where needed
• No reliability penalty for verified app outages
• No requirement for premium personal devices
Adoption metrics
• Weekly active sitter adoption
• Booking acknowledgement
• Check-in/out completion
• Report submission
• Support contacts per sitter
• Time required to submit a report
• Device-specific failure rate
Risk 6: Customer app remains unused
A customer app should initially target:
• Repeat customers
• Active subscribers
• Society-plan users

### Page 576

• Customers who regularly track services
• Multi-pet households
The strongest value proposition is not “we now have an app.” It is:
• Faster repeat booking
• Live service status
• Report history
• Subscription self-service
• Remaining-credit visibility
• Preferred-sitter management
• Better support
Do not force installation. Continue to support the PWA, deep links and assisted booking.
Risk 7: Incorrect AI matching
AI matching must remain an admin decision-support tool.
Hard eligibility rules
↓
Explainable candidate ranking
↓
Top three recommendations
↓
Admin approval
↓
Sitter acceptance
The model must never override:
• Expired documentation
• Pet-risk incompatibility
• Sitter suspension
• Service-zone restrictions
• Capacity limits

### Page 577

• Society-access requirements
• Schedule conflicts
Required governance
• Display a match score, not an uncalibrated probability
• Explain positive and negative factors
• Record algorithm version
• Record admin override reasons
• Monitor new-sitter exposure
• Audit assignment and earnings concentration
• Require human approval for all assignments initially
• Never auto-assign high-risk cases
Initial success targets
Metric Target
Top-three list includes selected sitter 80%+
Human approval 100%
High-risk auto-assignments Zero
False eligibility exclusions Near zero
Recommendation outcome audit Monthly
Risk 8: Wallet and credit disputes
The early product should be called Credits, not Wallet.
Use:
• Subscription service credits
• Referral credits
• Promotional credits
• Society benefits
• Service-recovery credits
Do not initially support:

### Page 578

• Customer-funded cash storage
• Transfers between users
• Cash withdrawal
• Open merchant usage
RBI distinguishes closed-system instruments used only for the issuer’s own services from
broader prepaid instruments, and closed systems cannot be used for third-party
settlement or cash withdrawal. A marketplace with independent providers should obtain
specialist payments advice before introducing a cash-like stored-value product.
Ledger requirements
Every credit movement must be append-only:
GRANT
RESERVE
CONSUME
RELEASE
RESTORE
EXPIRE
REVERSE
ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT
Every transaction needs:
• Idempotency key
• Reason
• Source booking/subscription
• Actor
• Expiry
• Timestamp
• Reversal link where applicable
Risk 9: App-store rejection
Store preparation should start while the apps are being built—not in the final week.
Common rejection areas

### Page 579

• Background location lacks a core use case
• Permission disclosure is vague
• Screenshots show unavailable features
• Demo account fails
• Privacy policy does not match actual SDK behaviour
• Account deletion is missing
• App crashes or has incomplete flows
• Data Safety or App Privacy declarations are inaccurate
Apple requires apps supporting account creation to let users initiate account deletion
inside the app. Google Play requires an in-app deletion path and an external web resource
for account-deletion requests.
Apple requires developers to disclose data collected by the application and integrated
third-party partners. Google Play similarly requires accurate Data Safety declarations and
may take enforcement action when app behaviour conflicts with the declaration.
Risk 10: Push notifications are treated as guaranteed delivery
Push is useful but should not be the only operational channel.
Firebase recommends storing token timestamps, refreshing tokens and removing stale or
invalid registrations.
For critical events use:
Backend event
+ in-app inbox
+ push
+ operations queue
+ SMS/call fallback where necessary
Do not define “push reliability” only as messages submitted to FCM or APNs.
Measure:
• Active-token coverage
• Provider acceptance
• Application receipt where measurable

### Page 580

• User acknowledgement
• Fallback activation
• Stale-token rate
2. Phase 11 upgrade decisions
Upgrade 1: Build the sitter app before the customer app
Decision: APPROVE — NON-NEGOTIABLE
The sitter app directly improves:
• Booking acknowledgement
• Arrival reliability
• Service evidence
• GPS tracking
• Report quality
• Incident response
• Payout transparency
The customer app should follow after the sitter workflow and command centre are
operationally stable.
Upgrade 2: Use React Native with Expo
Decision: APPROVE AS THE OFFICIAL DEFAULT STACK
As of 2026, the official React Native documentation recommends using a framework such
as Expo for new applications and describes Expo as a production-grade React Native
framework.
Approved stack
Layer Decision
Mobile framework React Native with Expo development builds
Language TypeScript
Navigation Expo Router
Backend Existing Node.js/Next.js APIs

### Page 581

Layer Decision
Database PostgreSQL/PostGIS
Push Expo Notifications with FCM/APNs
Secure token storage Platform-backed secure storage
Offline queue SQLite/local outbox
Error monitoring Sentry
Product analytics PostHog or Firebase with redaction
Build/release EAS or equivalent CI/CD
Condition
Run native proofs for:
• Background GPS
• App termination
• Push deep links
• Offline media
• Battery impact
• Android OEM behaviour
• iOS background execution
Expo development builds are required for background location and other production native
behaviour that Expo Go does not support.
Upgrade 3: AI remains admin-approved
Decision: APPROVE — MANDATORY FOR THE INITIAL RELEASE
AI should recommend candidates and explain the ranking. It should not autonomously
assign every booking.
Human approval is especially required for:
• High-risk pets
• Medication
• Senior or specialist care

### Page 582

• First boarding
• Previous incidents
• Multiple large pets
• Unusual home access
• No established backup
Upgrade 4: Track location only during active service
Decision: APPROVE — NON-NEGOTIABLE
The official policy should state:
No active booking
→ no tracking
Service explicitly started
→ tracking permitted
Service completed or aborted
→ tracking stops
This must be enforced technically, not only written in the privacy policy.
Upgrade 5: Beta-first app launch
Decision: APPROVE — NON-NEGOTIABLE
Official progression:
Stage Users Exit purpose
Internal alpha Team and QA Functional and security validation
Sitter beta 20–50 sitters Field-execution validation
Customer beta 100–500 loyal users Booking and retention validation
City rollout One validated city Production stability

6. Final Phase 11 recommendation
Build:
A sitter-first mobile operating system connected to an advanced command centre,
followed by a customer application for repeated booking, live service visibility,
subscriptions, credits and preferred-caregiver continuity.
Do
• Build sitter execution first.
• Use React Native with Expo development builds.
• Validate GPS on real Android and iOS devices early.
• Keep AI recommendations explainable and admin-approved.
• Use an immutable service-credit ledger.
• Launch through controlled cohorts.
• Keep the PWA operational.
• Maintain kill switches and rollback controls.
• Treat security and privacy testing as continuous engineering work.
Do not
• Track sitters outside active services.

### Page 592

• Build a cash-like wallet without regulatory analysis.
• auto-assign high-risk bookings.
• depend on push alone for emergency communication.
• expose exact customer data before assignment.
• publish both apps to every city simultaneously.
• add social, insurance or e-commerce distractions.
• call app downloads the primary success metric.
Final approval matrix
Phase 11 rule Decision
Build sitter app before customer app APPROVED
Use React Native with Expo APPROVED
AI matching remains admin-approved APPROVED
Location tracking only during active service APPROVED — NON-NEGOTIABLE
App launch remains beta-first APPROVED — NON-NEGOTIABLE
Phase 11 win condition
Sitters execute services more reliably, operations detect failures earlier, customers
gain trustworthy visibility and self-service controls, and repeat users adopt the apps
without increasing safety incidents, privacy exposure or financial disputes.
Phase 11 final decision
GO — with all five rules approved.
Move to Phase 12: Brand, Community and Content Engine only after the sitter app,
customer app, GPS system, credit ledger, notifications and command centre pass the
measurable gates above.
